In the lock snapshots the GPU pool slots 0–12 are held before the click and released after it, slot 13 stays, and `spawn.play-slot.0` is taken after it. So another holder on the same origin takes the renderer slot, and the local renderer is removed. On live the outer page and the kernel frame are on different origins, so they do not share the locks. | 142 | DEFECT, stop rule (recorded at the owner's request). What happened: the stop step at the end of the cd8a19b run listed listener PIDs by a port match on ':4173' and ':9333' with grep, and grep's ':4173' also matches ':41733'. The match caught `node scripts/witness.mjs` (PID 2995643), a thebird witness run started by another gm worker, and its child tree; my loop sent SIGTERM to all of them. Killed: 13 processes of that tree (chromium and esbuild children). Still alive: PID 2995643 (not signalled again). Cause: the stop step selected processes by port and by name, not by the run's own recorded PIDs, and it walked child trees from PIDs it did not record. Rule, from now on: stop only PIDs that this run started, found by walking the child tree down from the PID the run recorded (browser.pid and server.pid in the run directory); never match by port, never signal a process from a name or port match alone. Fix: .spawn/local/stop-run.sh takes the run directory, checks that each recorded PID's command line belongs to this run (its profile path for the browser, serve-local.mjs for the server), and signals only that PID's group and walked tree. Test: .spawn/local/test-stop-run.sh starts its own tree and an unrelated decoy; PASS requires the tree stopped, the decoy alive and refused. Verified (test PASS) | test-stop-run.sh output, PID 2995643 still running | Defect recorded; fixed by stop-run.sh; the thebird processes already stopped are not recoverable |

| 161 | STEP 1, THE WORKER'S FRAME CLOCK (Phase A, method). Source read: the renderer's frame loop (served a0fe32d7-32342e395a0c0bd8.mjs) is a rAF callback GFA, armed by Dk through self.requestAnimationFrame (or a GlassFrameClock that calls it); when rAF is absent the timer dm5 (armed by ih through setTimeout) draws the frame (gy counts timer-driven frames). The frame draw Qe takes the rAF arrival stamp e and stores it in $5r.arrivalStampMs (module scope, not exposed). The probe (workerInit) now records: (a) each rAF arrival, with the callback name, the arrival timestamp t and performance.now() (P.frameClock, k 'raf'); (b) each timer-driven frame dm5, with its delay and time (k 'timer'); (c) each frame post (self.postMessage of kind frame-bitmap) with frameId, time, submits since the last post, and the fingerprint (FNV over the first 4 KB) of each writeBuffer since the last post (P.framePosts, fps); (d) the first four float32 values of the largest writeBuffer since the last post (big.f). Verified by node --check only; witness is Phase B. Open | probe-scene.js workerInit | Verified (probe-1795069578) |
| 162 | STEP 2, THE SCENE'S OWN TIME (method and exposure). The renderer does not expose its scene time on globalThis: the frame arrival stamp is $5r.arrivalStampMs[0] and the step bookkeeping (Jf, wO, Ql, gy, tx) is module-scoped in a0fe32d7, so no probe can read them directly. The probe that exposes the scene's time per frame is the uniform writes (row 161, d): if the scene's time is in a uniform buffer, one of the four leading float32 values advances with the frame clock. Step 2 therefore identifies that float from the data (a float that rises monotonically across frames at the rAF rate), and says plainly if none does. Open | probe-scene.js (d) | Open |
| 163 | DECISION RULE (fixed before the witness, so it is not read from the result). STALL: the frame loop stops (no rAF or timer entry for longer than 2 s while frame posts stop, or frameId stops increasing), OR the scene's time float stops rising while the loop keeps arriving. STEADY STATE: the loop arrives at a regular cadence, frameId increases on every post, and the scene's time float keeps rising, while the image hash repeats (repetition with time advancing). The cause is named from the same data (for a stall, the step where the loop or the time stops; for a steady state, the input that does not change the image). Open | probe-scene.js, rule applied in row 164 | Open |
| 164 | MEASUREMENT FINDINGS SO FAR (probe-1795069578, forced bitmap, headers on, keep-alive). (a) The frame clock: 400 entries cap reached at 8.1 s, so the clock log did not cover the posts (11.6 s to 46.4 s): 43 frame posts, frameId 1 to 43, one per transfer (43). The loop's arrivals in the first 8 s: 261 GFA rAF, 7 onBeat rAF (the GlassFrameClock), 132 timer dm5 fallback frames. The cap is raised (row 165). (b) Uniform fingerprints differ between consecutive posts on every frame (fp! in all 42 pairs), including the pairs where the image is the same (img= in 36 pairs). So the fingerprint of the writes is not a usable state check (writes per frame vary: 11 to 47). (c) Scene time source: the renderer builds the scene from the frame object with timeSeconds = frameNowMs/1000, worldTimeSeconds, deltaSeconds = dtMs/1000 (a0fe32d7 at the graph build). The frame's time stamp is $5r.arrivalStampMs[0] = the rAF arrival (Qe). Whether that time reaches the GPU is measured in row 165 by searching the uniform floats for a value equal to the arrival time in seconds. Search for the scene's time in the per-write floats (fs): for each post, every float32 value v is compared with each rAF arrival stamp t (ms) as v*1000 against t within 2 ms, and the (write index, float index) pairs that match in consecutive posts are counted. | 171 | THE VERDICT, STALL OR STEADY STATE (rule of row 163, applied). The rule's stall test (the loop stops, or the scene time stops while the loop arrives) is only partly met. The loop does not stop: arrivals and frame posts continue to the last sample in every run, and the fallback timer carries the loop at 25 to 60 callbacks per second when reads are off (row 170). But arrival gaps above 2 s occur (3.3 to 3.7 s, four to six per run), and the identical-frame runs mostly do not overlap them: probe-1795070144, runs of 15, 13, 3 and 7 frames, the 15-frame run overlaps one gap, the others none; probe-1795070000, runs of 6, 22 and 2, one gap overlaps the 22-frame run only at its end. So the repeats are mostly not loop stops; they sit inside a low callback rate (1 to 3 per second). The steady-state test (the scene time keeps advancing) cannot be made: the scene time is not exposed (row 173). VERDICT: not a loop stall; steady state not established (time half open). Verified (arrival gaps and image runs from the same scene.json) | probe-1795070144, probe-1795070000 | Verified (not a stall of the loop; time open) |
| 172 | THE CAUSE OF THE LOW FRAME CADENCE, MEASURED. The probe's own per-transfer readback is a large part of it: the worker reads each transferred bitmap back and hashes it, 737.6 ms per transfer (row 170, probe-1795070319). With the reads off (READ=0, probe-1795070470) the frame posts rise from 45 to 101 in about the same time, so the probe slows the frame path by about half. The rAF collapse (worker GFA arrivals falling to 0 to 8 per second) remains with the reads off, and begins at the first presented frame (9 s in probe-1795070470, 9 to 12 s in the others). The page's own rAF does not collapse: the session counts 10 to 50 rAF per second through the run (probe-1795070000, sessRaf). So the worker's rAF is starved while the page's is not. The mechanism is not measured: the renderer's frame clock (GlassFrameClock fed by the page's display clock, or self.requestAnimationFrame) is not recorded per request. The adapter is a software fallback (env.adapterInfo: swiftshader, isFallbackAdapter true), so the frame work is CPU-rendered and a GPU-fence wait is a candidate for the post-level limit (the renderer logs 'held by the GPU fence'); it is not yet measured (row 174). Verified (reads and rates) / open (mechanism) | probe-1795070319, probe-1795070470, probe-1795070000 scene.json; env.adapterInfo | Verified (readback cost); open (rAF starvation mechanism) |
| 173 | THE SCENE'S OWN TIME, NOT EXPOSED TO THE PROBE. The renderer builds the scene from the frame object with timeSeconds = frameNowMs/1000, worldTimeSeconds and deltaSeconds = dtMs/1000 (a0fe32d7, the graph build), and the frame stamp is $5r.arrivalStampMs[0] (module scope). The probe searched 36 frame posts, each with the 32 float32 values of every uniform write (about 128 bytes per write, 20 to 80 writes per post, probe-1795069852): one value matches an arrival stamp in one post (2:28, count 1), which is not a time track. So the time is either not in the first 128 bytes of any write, or not in the uniforms at all. To expose it: scan every write's full byte range for a float that follows the arrival stamp (a larger scan), or read the frame object at the graph build, which is module-internal and needs the served module to expose it. | 175 | SCENE-TIME SCAN, EVERY BYTE (Phase A, 4a417fb). Every writeBuffer is scanned at every byte offset (k = 0 to n-4, DataView getFloat32, little-endian) for a float32 whose value times 1000 is within 50 ms of the latest arrival stamp (the last rAF arrival's timestamp t, or the last timer frame's time). Each hit is stored with its byte offset k, value, and dt (ms), and the write is labelled by its buffer (the createBuffer label, size and usage, via a WeakMap). The probe no longer caps writes at 80 per frame (cap 400). The post carries hw (per write: label, byte length, hits). Test: a hit at the same (label, k) in most posts with dt near zero in all of them is a field that tracks the arrival stamp; no such track is a finding that no byte of any uniform write tracks it. Limit: the scan reads whole write payloads (dataOffset and size arguments are not applied), and only float32 fields at byte offsets, so a field stored as another type is not seen. Witness: Phase B. Open | probe-scene.js (writeBuffer, clockPush, createBuffer) | Open (no frames in Phase B, row 183) |

It returns false only when the sink is null or the transfer throws; the throw path closes the bitmap and reaches the uf catch (warn: bitmap paint failed). | 227 | PROBE HOOK FOR THE BITMAP-ROAD CANVAS, PHASE A (no run, no push). HOOK: in .spawn/local/probe-scene.js, workerInit. (1) GPUCommandEncoder.prototype.beginRenderPass wrapper now calls noteCanvasPass(enc, desc) and returns enc unchanged. noteCanvasPass looks up each color attachment's view to its texture (viewTex) and that texture's canvas (texCanvas, set by the getCurrentTexture wrapper). If a pass targets a canvas, it wraps the returned encoder's end() so that, at end, it records { loads, stores, clears, draws } where draws is the delta of P.draw + P.drawIndexed across the pass, and queues the record per canvas in pendingPasses. (2) OffscreenCanvas.prototype.transferToImageBitmap wrapper takes the queued passes for this canvas, clears the queue, and pushes P.worldTransfers { n, f: P.submit, decision, passes, draws, clears, loads, stores }; it then calls the original transfer and returns the bitmap unchanged. It reads no pixels, so its added work is bookkeeping only; the existing sendBitmap read still runs only when READ equals 1. (3) workerCounters adds world: the last six worldTransfers records. FIELDS: n is the transfer index; f is P.submit, used as a frame index proxy (count of submits, not a true frame id). DECISION RULE per transfer: no-pass when no canvas pass was queued since the last transfer; drawn when any queued pass has draws greater than zero, meaning the world was drawn before the transfer; cleared-or-released when passes were queued and all had zero draws, meaning the canvas was cleared or its texture released before the transfer. RED CHECK (harness /tmp/verify/hook_redcheck.mjs, outside the repo, extracts workerInit from the file and runs it under vm with stubs): intact hook gives records drawn (1 pass, 1 draw), no-pass, cleared-or-released (1 pass, 0 draws), recordCount 2. The sabotaged copy, which drops the queue push, gives three no-pass records and recordCount 0. The sabotage was applied in memory only; the file is unchanged. RESTORE CHECK: git status shows only .spawn/local/probe-scene.js modified before this commit. LIMITS: the hook is in the broker worker, and a worker that closes before the late patch will not report world records; the page side cannot see worker P, so the record is valid only when workerCounters reads a live handle. The transfer happens in the worker because the kernel's bitmap road transfers an OffscreenCanvas-backed WebGPU canvas; the HTMLCanvasElement form in the request does not apply to this path. NEXT (Phase B, not run): one gated probe run with READ=0 on the forced bitmap command, with the vmstat 1 2 swap reading recorded first; quote the world records and push only if a record matches one of the three cases with its witness. | probe-scene.js workerInit: beginRenderPass wrapper, noteCanvasPass block after texCanvas, transfer wrapper, workerCounters world field | red check: recordCount 2 intact, 0 sabotaged; no browser launched; Phase B not run. |

| 262 | HOLDER SMAPS SNAPSHOT (Phase A, no browser run, no push). CHANGE: .spawn/local/holder-proc.mjs parses every /proc/<pid>/smaps mapping in one read pass, keys each by start-end, and dedupes on that key (keeping the first). It sorts the deduped set by numeric start, and counts out-of-order entries in the raw file order and overlaps (a start below the previous end) in the sorted set. A pass with no overlaps is accepted; a pass with overlaps is re-read, up to 3 attempts, and the totals record attempts and consistent. Anonymous and file-backed sums come from the accepted pass. Totals gain duplicatesDropped, outOfOrder, overlaps, attempts, consistent, anonRollupDiffBytes (anonymousBytes minus smaps_rollup Anonymous) and rssRollupDiffBytes (anon plus file RSS minus rollup Rss), so a disagreement is reported as a number and not hidden. Why: the row 258 top-20 had 7 entries sharing a start with an earlier entry (same VMA, different end), which exact dedupe cannot catch, so the overlap check and bounded re-read are needed as well. The reader is a bounded retry, not a guarantee: if 3 passes all overlap, consistent is false and the sums are reported as read. | holder-proc.mjs diff | Done; the witness and red checks are row 263. |

| 273 | HOLDER MEMORY-INFRA DUMP REFUSED: READER GUARD FIX (Phase A, no browser run, no push). CAUSE, FROM THE RUN (row 272, probe-1795135701): the dump reply came after 15.2 s with dumpGuid 0x4 and success false, and no further CDP event before the reader's 20 s guard, so eventsSeen 0. The reply has no reason field, and the browser log has no memory-dump line. WHAT THE CALLS SAY: requestMemoryDump is a global request on the browser target (protocol.d.ts: parameters deterministic and levelOfDetail only, no process argument; returns dumpGuid and success, 'true iff the global memory dump succeeded'), so no per-process request exists and the reader's browser-socket request is the correct target. The binary's strings include the skip rule 'skipped because another dump request with the same level of detail', which answers quickly, but the reply took 15.2 s, so this request was not that skip (inference). The per-process outcome names are not in the strings, so the refusal reason for the 15 s attempt is not identified. WHAT CHANGED: the reader keeps its events when success is false (already so) and waits for tracingComplete up to 40 s, not 20 s; bound-watch.sh gives the memdump line 45 s, not 25 s, so the watchdog does not kill it first. This is the only code path that can drop data after a refused reply. The witness that reproduces the failure is row 274. | holder-memdump.mjs (guard 40000), bound-watch.sh (timeout 45) | Done; the witness RED on the old reader, GREEN on this one. | 278 | STANDALONE DUMP CHECK: DOES THE REFUSAL APPLY TO THE HOLDER OR TO EVERY DUMP (Phase A, no browser run, no push). QUESTION: the row 272 to 276 dumps were all refused at 15 s with success false, and only the holder was measured; a refusal for every dump means the browser refuses the global request; a refusal only for the holder means the holder's load is the cause. METHOD (.spawn/local/standalone-dump.mjs, port and load MB as arguments): opens an about:blank target on the browser socket, attaches it flattened, allocates the load in that page (300 Uint8Array of 1 MB, which the array_buffer partition holds), starts tracing with the reader's config, requests one detailed dump with no probe running, and records the reply and its time, the Tracing.end send and reply times, and the tracingComplete time. It then reads which pids have dump events, each pid's partition total, array_buffer size and RSS, the load pid (the pid whose array_buffer is at least 90 percent of the load), and a scope: none (success), per-process (refused, but processes still dumped), or global (refused, nothing dumped). It closes the target and prints STANDALONE_DUMP. Tracing.end is not awaited, as in row 276. | .spawn/local/standalone-dump.mjs | Done in fixture (row 279); the browser run is the Phase B step. |

| 310 | ITEM 1, H2 CONTROL AND WITNESS: THE PROBE FLUSH WITHOUT A DUMP (Phase A, no browser run, no push). CHANGE: holder-memdump.mjs reads HOLDER_MODE. 'no-dump' skips Tracing.requestMemoryDump, holds the trace open for 3000 ms, then sends Tracing.end; out.mode records dump or no-dump. | 336 | WHY THE SAVED TRACE STOPS AT +133 ms (item 1 of this stretch; diagnosis from current source and the saved events of probe-1795160684; NO code change, no probe run). FROM SOURCE: the reader (holder-memdump.mjs) keeps every Tracing.dataCollected value without filtering (events.push of m.params.value) and writes the array at finish; it keeps bufferUsage samples on the same socket. The protocol (types/protocol.d.ts in the local patchright-core) says: bufferUsage percentFull is the fraction of the event buffer used; traceBufferSizeInKb defaults to 200 MB; with transferMode ReportEvents, dataCollected is a sequence sent when tracing is stopped, followed by tracingComplete. NO Chromium source is in this workspace (checked; the Chromium writer, TraceLog, perfetto and the DevTools tracing handler are not available), so the trace writer and flush code cannot be read here. FROM THE SAVED EVENTS: (1) recording continued: percentFull rose about 7 percent per second (7.1 at +1 s, 14.6 at +2 s, 99.9 from +13 s), about 14 MB per second of the 200 MB default, so the browser's central buffer kept filling after the cut; (2) delivery stopped: the saved events end at +133.1 ms for pid 2359065 (26,047 events) and for the holder 2359201 (16,059 events), at +130.0 ms for the browser 2359019, and no dataCollected event arrived after; (3) the stop is shared across processes: the two large renderer-side pids end at the same instant (133.1 ms), so the cut is not one process freezing on its own; the small pids end earlier because they recorded less; (4) the socket stayed alive: bufferUsage arrived every second to +15 s, so the reader was receiving messages, and the reader's own collection is unfiltered. CAN DISTINGUISH: the cut is in delivery (dataCollected stops while recording and sampling continue), it is shared across processes, and it coincides with the end of the holder's dump providers (last provider +131.8 ms). CANNOT DISTINGUISH from these events: whether the browser's DevTools tracing handler stopped sending during the memory dump, or a renderer's trace agent stopped flushing its chunks to the browser (both would look like this); whether the cut is caused by the dump itself or only coincides with it; whether events after +133 ms exist in the buffer (the buffer's percent says data was recorded, not what it contains). NEXT EXPERIMENT (proposed, not run): Tracing.start with transferMode ReturnAsStream and reading the stream with IO.read after Tracing.end, which uses a different delivery path; it would separate a DevTools-handler stop from a renderer flush stop, and needs a reader change (Phase A with witness and red check) before a probe. | docs/PENDING_VALIDATION.md; probe-1795160684/holder-events.json; bound.holder | Open: decision for the owner: approve the ReturnAsStream experiment (a reader change and one gated window). |

The holder's MemoryDumpManager::FinishAsyncProcessDump is never recorded (no X and no B), and the one B FinishAsyncProcessDump for pid 2359100 has no end. So the stuck point is the holder's dump-finish step, which runs after its last provider and before its per-process result reaches the browser; the flush that would carry the trace past that point never completes (tracingComplete missing). WHICH PROCESS OWNS THE BLOCK IS NOT ESTABLISHED: the saved events cannot name the process that fails to acknowledge the trace stop, because the stop itself never completes; the holder is the strongest candidate (its own events stop with the others at the same instant). ORDER NOTE: the reader sends Tracing.end only after the dump reply, so the trace stop is always issued after the 15 s deadline, which means nothing can be delivered from the stop path when the dump fails. CHANGE (Phase A, done): holder-memdump.mjs now records a reason field whenever a dump does not finish: reasonOf() returns dump-reply-missing (no reply to requestMemoryDump), trace-stop-never-completed (no tracingComplete), holder-finish-absent (tracing completed, no holder FinishAsyncProcessDump), or dump-reply-false (the holder finish present and the reply still false); null when the dump succeeds or mode is not dump. The finish() function records holderFinishEvents (the holder's FinishAsyncProcessDump events in the saved trace) before writing HOLDER_MEMDUMP. The reason is classified from what the reader observes; the protocol has no reason field (the real reply carries dumpGuid and success only, row 335). Open, not verified:

- Zero `ERR_ABORTED` (single tab and concurrent tabs). Not reached. Per-load counts with the 503 grant ranged from 7 to 58 in the current state and 41 to 82 before the closure capture; the trace is in `docs/LOCAL-RUN.md`. Documented as known kernel behaviour.
- The 3D world does not draw, in the headless run (row 18) or the headed run (row 22). The headed run has a real NVIDIA adapter (row 21), so the adapter is not the blocker: the kernel never requests its renderer modules, and the snapshot is missing the world content those requests need (row 22). The earlier headless measurement: WebGL works (SwiftShader), but `navigator.gpu.requestAdapter()` returns `null`, and the installed playwriter launches Chromium with `chromium.launch({ headless: true, executablePath })` and no `args` (`playwriter/dist/executor.js`, around line 830). No installed setting reaches that call: `PLAYWRITER_WEBGPU` is not read, `AGENT_GRAPHICS` is kitty output, and `HEADFUL` is test-only. The gm change needed: read `PLAYWRITER_CHROMIUM_ARGS` (a JSON array of strings) and pass it as `args` at that call. The contract and its check are in `docs/LOCAL-RUN.md`. Which flags produce a WebGPU adapter here is not verified; that needs the change and a fresh browser.
- The `ready-plate` stub is local only. The kernel does not need the hosted content: a `404`, the empty `200`, and a snapshot-derived fragment give the same page state (see the table in `docs/LOCAL-RUN.md`).

Tried and not kept:

- Serve-time cancel removal in the kernel's door-ask let-go (`b7` in `_v4_client_pool_60d2a80e`, and the same string in `f844f1a1`): the cancel `$.asking?.controller.abort(...)` was stripped from the served text. The trace showed it was the only JS-initiated abort on the `?wave=whole` loads, but after the strip single-tab stayed at 81 and concurrent went to 40 and 50. The unpatched code then gave 77 single-tab and 41 and 50 concurrent, so the change had no measurable effect. Reverted; `.spawn/local/serve-local.mjs` matches HEAD.

- A localizer that answers same-path session writes with `history.replaceState` instead of navigating: the kernel does not write through `iframe.src`, so it never ran. Reverted.
- Rewriting `cache:"reload"` to `cache:"default"`: the same aborts remained. Reverted.
- Removing the eager kernel frame's `src`: the aborts went away, but the session never loaded.
